Ran terminal command: @'
import re
from pathlib import Path
root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\2')
source = root / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_13m32s.gcode'
saved_z = 3.04
number = r"[+-]?(?:\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+)"
parameter_pattern = re.compile(rf"(?:^|\s)([A-Z])\s*({number})(?=\s|$)", re.IGNORECASE)
motion_pattern = re.compile(r"^G(?:0|00|1|01)(?:\s|$)", re.IGNORECASE)
motions = []
for index, line in enumerate(source.read_text(errors='replace').splitlines()):
    command = line.split(';', 1)[0].strip().upper()
    if motion_pattern.match(command):
        parameters = {key.upper(): float(value) for key, value in parameter_pattern.findall(command)}
        motions.append((index, command, parameters))
saved_matches = [item for item in motions if 'Z' in item[2] and abs(item[2]['Z'] - saved_z) <= .005]
assert saved_matches, f'No saved Z={saved_z} move in fixture'
last_saved_index = saved_matches[-1][0]
height = next((item for item in motions if item[0] > last_saved_index and item[2].get('Z', saved_z) > saved_z + .005), None)
assert height, 'No later layer height found'
xy = next((item for item in motions if item[0] > height[0] and 'X' in item[2] and 'Y' in item[2]), None)
assert xy, 'No XY travel found after next layer height'
assert height[2]['Z'] > saved_z and xy[0] > height[0]
print(f'PASS: saved Z {saved_z:g}; next layer Z {height[2]["Z"]:g}; first XY move line {xy[0] + 1}: {xy[1]}')
'@ | python -

Ran terminal command: @'
import re
from pathlib import Path
root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\2')
source = root / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_13m32s.gcode'
saved_z = 3.04
number = r"[+-]?(?:\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+)"
parameter_pattern = re.compile(rf"(?:^|\s)([A-Z])\s*({number})(?=\s|$)", re.IGNORECASE)
motion_pattern = re.compile(r"^G(?:0|00|1|01)(?:\s|$)", re.IGNORECASE)
motions = []
for index, line in enumerate(source.read_text(errors='replace').splitlines()):
    command = line.split(';', 1)[0].strip().upper()
    if motion_pattern.match(command):
        parameters = {key.upper(): float(value) for key, value in parameter_pattern.findall(command)}
        motions.append((index, command, parameters))
saved_matches = [item for item in motions if 'Z' in item[2] and abs(item[2]['Z'] - saved_z) <= .005]
assert saved_matches, f'No saved Z={saved_z} move in fixture'
last_saved_index = saved_matches[-1][0]
height = next((item for item in motions if item[0] > last_saved_index and item[2].get('Z', saved_z) > saved_z + .005), None)
assert height, 'No later layer height found'
travel = next((item for item in motions if item[0] >= height[0] and 'X' in item[2] and 'Y' in item[2] and 'E' not in item[2]), None)
assert travel, 'No non-extruding XY move at or after next layer height'
assert travel[0] < next(item[0] for item in motions if item[0] > travel[0] and 'E' in item[2]), 'Expected travel before extrusion'
print(f'PASS: saved Z {saved_z:g}; next layer Z {height[2]["Z"]:g}; resume travel line {travel[0] + 1}: {travel[1]}')
'@ | python -

The revised selection now lands on the fixtureâ€™s first safe XY travel at Z 3.24, not the first extrusion. Iâ€™m doing an end-to-end local run of the embedded generator against that G-code and will confirm the generated recovery file has the homing setup and expected continuation.

Ran terminal command: @'
import sys
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\2')
script = (root / 'plr' / 'plr.sh').read_text()
marker = "python3 - "$filepath" "$resume_z" "$PLR_PATH/$(basename "$last_file")" <<'PY'\n"
body = script.split(marker, 1)[1].split("\nPY\n", 1)[0]
source = root / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_13m32s.gcode'
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
    output = Path(temp_dir) / source.name
    sys.argv = ['plr-generator', str(source), '3.04', str(output)]
    exec(compile(body, 'plr-generator', 'exec'))
    generated = output.read_text().splitlines()
    assert generated[0] == 'SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION Z=3.04 SET_HOMED=XYZ', generated[0]
    assert generated[1:5] == ['G91', 'G1 Z10 F600', 'G90', 'G28 X Y'], generated[1:5]
    assert generated[13].startswith('G1 X119.607 Y119.607 Z3.24'), generated[13]
    assert generated[-1].strip() == 'M73 P100 R0' or any('X100.393 Y119.607 E.6471' in line for line in generated)
    print(f'PASS: generated {output.name} with explicit homing and resumed at layer Z=3.24')
'@ | python -

Ran terminal command: @'
import sys
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path

root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\2')
script_text = (root / 'plr' / 'plr.sh').read_text()
marker = "python3 - "$filepath" "$resume_z" "$PLR_PATH/$(basename "$last_file")" <<'PY'\n"
body = script_text.split(marker, 1)[1].split("\nPY\n", 1)[0]
source_path = root / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_13m32s.gcode'
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
    generated_path = Path(temp_dir) / source_path.name
    sys.argv = ['plr-generator', str(source_path), '3.04', str(generated_path)]
    exec(compile(body, 'plr-generator', 'exec'), {})
    generated_lines = generated_path.read_text().splitlines()
    assert generated_lines[0] == 'SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION Z=3.04 SET_HOMED=XYZ', generated_lines[0]
    assert generated_lines[1:5] == ['G91', 'G1 Z10 F600', 'G90', 'G28 X Y'], generated_lines[1:5]
    assert generated_lines[10:12] == ['G1 X119.607 Y119.607 F6000', 'G1 Z3.24 F600'], generated_lines[10:12]
    assert generated_lines[15].startswith('G1 X119.607 Y119.607 Z3.24 F21000'), generated_lines[15]
    assert any(line == 'G1 X100.393 Y119.607 E.6471' for line in generated_lines)
    print(f'PASS: generated recovery file; explicit homing, safe layer travel, and later extrusion are present')

Use a small test print, not a valuable print. Copy these folder `6` files to the printer first: `printer_data/config/plr.cfg`, `printer_data/config/macros.cfg`, and `printer_data/plr/plr.sh`. Then run `FIRMWARE_RESTART` and wait for Klipper to become ready.

import sys

body = script_text.split(marker, 1)[1].split("\nPY\n", 1)[0]
source_path = root / 'gcodes' / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_13m14s.gcode'
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
    output_path = Path(temp_dir) / source_path.name
    sys.argv = ['plr-generator', str(source_path), '2.44', str(output_path), 'Cube_id_0_copy_0']
    exec(compile(body, 'plr-generator', 'exec'), {})
    generated = output_path.read_text().splitlines()
    assert generated[0] == 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE RESET=1', generated[:5]
    assert generated[1].startswith('EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE NAME=Cube_id_0_copy_0'), generated[:5]

    assert generated[3] == 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_START NAME=Cube_id_0_copy_0', generated[:5]
    assert any(line.startswith('G1 Z2.64') for line in generated[:12]), generated[:12]
    print('PASS: recovery output restores definitions, exclusion, and active object before resuming at Z=2.64')

import sys
import tempfile
from pathlib import Path
root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\7')
marker = "python3 - "$filepath" "$resume_z" "$PLR_PATH/$(basename "$last_file")" "$excluded_names" <<'PY'\n"

template_script = (root / 'KlipperPLR' / 'plr.sh').read_text()
active_body = active_script.split(marker, 1)[1].split("\nPY\n", 1)[0]
template_body = template_script.split(marker, 1)[1].split("\nPY\n", 1)[0]
assert active_body == template_body, 'Active and install-template Python generators differ'
compile(template_body, 'plr-generator', 'exec')
source_path = root / 'printer_data' / 'gcodes' / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_13m14s.gcode'
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
    output_path = Path(temp_dir) / source_path.name
    sys.argv = ['plr-generator', str(source_path), '2.44', str(output_path), 'CUBE_ID_0_COPY_0']
    exec(compile(template_body, 'plr-generator', 'exec'), {})
    generated = output_path.read_text().splitlines()
    assert generated[0] == 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE RESET=1', generated[:5]
    assert generated[1].startswith('EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE NAME=Cube_id_0_copy_0'), generated[:5]
    assert generated[2] == 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT NAME=CUBE_ID_0_COPY_0', generated[:5]
    assert generated[3] == 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_START NAME=Cube_id_0_copy_0', generated[:5]
    assert any(line.startswith('G1 Z2.64') for line in generated[:12]), generated[:12]
print('PASS: active/template generators match and recovery G-code restores excluded and active object state')

Ran terminal command: @'
from pathlib import Path
from types import SimpleNamespace
from textwrap import dedent
import jinja2
path = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\7\printer_data\config\plr.cfg')
text = path.read_text()
block = text.split('[gcode_macro EXCLUDE_OBJECT]\n', 1)[1].split('\n[gcode_macro EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE]', 1)[0]
template_text = dedent(block.split('gcode:\n', 1)[1])
template = jinja2.Environment().from_string(template_text)
def render(stored, params, current=''):
    printer = SimpleNamespace(
        save_variables=SimpleNamespace(variables={'plr_excluded_objects': stored}),
        exclude_object=SimpleNamespace(current_object=current))
    rawparams = ' '.join(f'{key}={value}' for key, value in params.items())
    return template.render(printer=printer, params=params, rawparams=rawparams)
cases = [
    ('', {'NAME': 'cube_id_0'}, '', 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE='"CUBE_ID_0"''),
    ('CUBE_ID_0 CUBE_ID_1', {'NAME': 'CUBE_ID_0', 'RESET': '1'}, '', 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE='"CUBE_ID_1"''),
    ('CUBE_ID_0', {'RESET': '1'}, '', 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE='""''),
    ('', {'CURRENT': '1'}, 'CUBE_ID_2', 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE='"CUBE_ID_2"''),
]
for stored, params, current, expected in cases:
    rendered = render(stored, params, current)
    assert expected in rendered, rendered
    assert 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_BASE' in rendered
print('PASS: exclude, unexclude, reset-all, and CURRENT exclusion persist expected names')
'@ | python -

Ran terminal command: @'
import sys
from pathlib import Path
from types import SimpleNamespace
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__import__('os').environ['TEMP']) / 'klipper_jinja_test'))
import jinja2
root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\7\printer_data\config')
config_text = (root / 'plr.cfg').read_text()

def macro_gcode(text, name):
    marker = '[gcode_macro ' + name + ']\n'
    section = text.split(marker, 1)[1].split('\n[', 1)[0]
    return section.split('gcode:\n', 1)[1]

def render(text, name, variables, params=None, rawparams=''):
    printer = SimpleNamespace(
        save_variables=SimpleNamespace(variables=variables),
        exclude_object=SimpleNamespace(current_object=variables.get('_current_object')))
    return jinja2.Environment().from_string(macro_gcode(text, name)).render(
        printer=printer, params=params or {}, rawparams=rawparams)

add = render(config_text, 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT', {'plr_excluded_objects': 'A'}, {'NAME': 'B'}, 'NAME=B')
assert 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_BASE NAME=B' in add
assert 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE="A B"' in add, add
remove = render(config_text, 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT', {'plr_excluded_objects': 'A B'}, {'NAME': 'A', 'RESET': '1'}, 'NAME=A RESET=1')
assert 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE="B"' in remove, remove
clear = render(config_text, 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT', {'plr_excluded_objects': 'A B'}, {'RESET': '1'}, 'RESET=1')
assert 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE=""' in clear, clear
current = render(config_text, 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT', {'plr_excluded_objects': '', '_current_object': 'PART_C'}, {'CURRENT': '1'}, 'CURRENT=1')
assert 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE="PART_C"' in current, current
reset_defs = render(config_text, 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE', {'plr_excluded_objects': 'A'}, {'RESET': '1'}, 'RESET=1')
assert 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE_BASE RESET=1' in reset_defs and 'SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=plr_excluded_objects VALUE=""' in reset_defs

from types import SimpleNamespace

import jinja2

config_text = (root / 'plr.cfg').read_text()

def macro_gcode(text, name):
    marker = '[gcode_macro ' + name + ']\n'
    section = text.split(marker, 1)[1].split('\n[', 1)[0]
    return section.split('gcode:\n', 1)[1]

def render(text, name, variables, params=None, rawparams=''):
    printer = SimpleNamespace(
        save_variables=SimpleNamespace(variables=variables),
        exclude_object=SimpleNamespace(current_object=variables.get('_current_object')))
    return jinja2.Environment().from_string(macro_gcode(text, name)).render(
        printer=printer, params=params or {}, rawparams=rawparams)

import os, re, sys
from pathlib import Path
sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(os.environ['TEMP']) / 'klipper_jinja_test'))
import jinja2
root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\7')
env = jinja2.Environment('{%', '%}', '{', '}')
for path in (root / 'printer_data' / 'config' / 'plr.cfg', root / 'KlipperPLR' / 'plr.cfg'):
    text = path.read_text()
    sections = re.split(r'(?m)^\[', text)
    compiled = 0
    for section in sections[1:]:
        heading, _, body = section.partition(']')
        if not heading.startswith('gcode_macro '):
            continue
        code_match = re.search(r'(?m)^gcode:\s*\n((?:^[ \t].*\n|^\s*\n)*)', body)
        if code_match:
            env.from_string(code_match.group(1))
            compiled += 1
    print(f'PASS: {path.name}: compiled {compiled} macro templates')

GitHub Copilot: After `G1 X58.3 Y239.0 Z3 F5000`, `M400` waits for that move to finish. Then the macro descends in 0.01 mm steps using `_MOVE_Z_DOWN`:

```gcode
G1 Z-0.01 F50
```

Klipperâ€™s `F` is mm/min, so `F50` is about **0.83 mm/s**. It makes up to 500 steps, stopping when the button is pressed. When pressed, it reports `Z-end button hit at ...` and the recommended Z offset.

User: ho to increase speed 0.01 to 0.05

GitHub Copilot: In `_MOVE_Z_DOWN`, change:

```gcode
G1 Z-0.01 F50
```

to:

```gcode
G1 Z-0.05 F50
```

That makes each step five times farther; it does **not** increase speed. With the current 500-loop limit, maximum travel would grow from 5 mm to 25 mm. To keep the same 5 mm limit, also change `z_count` from `500` to `100`.

For faster movement without larger steps, keep `Z-0.01` and increase `F50` instead. Larger steps can overshoot the buttonâ€™s trigger position, so `0.01` is more precise.

This file is a Klipper PLR setup, most likely for Power Loss Recovery / Resume After Power Cut. The config in `plr.cfg` is defining macros and shell commands to remember the last print, store the last Z height, and resume a file after interruption.

What each section does:

- [force_move]
  - `enable_force_move: True`
  - This lets Klipper move the axes even when the printer thinks it is not homed or has lost position. Useful for recovery after a power cut.

- [delayed_gcode KINEMATIC_POSITION]
  - It runs at startup and does:
    - `SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION X=0 Y=0 Z=0`
  - This resets the internal position to zero after boot. It is often used to avoid bad positional state after a power loss.

- [respond]
  - `default_type: echo`
  - Makes generic responses show in console.

- [gcode_macro G31]
  - Calls `RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=clear_plr`
  - This likely clears PLR state when a G31 is triggered.

- [gcode_shell_command clear_plr]
  - Executes:
    - `sh {PLR_DIR}/clear_plr.sh`
  - So it runs an external shell script stored in `PLR_DIR`. This is probably a cleanup/reset script for previous recovery data.

- [gcode_macro save_last_file]
  - Saves the current print file name and path into `SAVE_VARIABLE`:
    - `last_file`
    - `filepath`
  - It reads:
    - `printer.virtual_sdcard.file_path`
    - `printer.virtual_sdcard.file_path.split('/')`
  - Then it stores the last filename. This is important for resuming the same print after a power issue.

- [gcode_macro clear_last_file]
  - Clears the stored values.
  - This resets the stored last file and path.

- [gcode_shell_command POWER_LOSS_RESUME]
  - Runs:
    - `/bin/bash /home/rangarajan/printer_data/plr/plr.sh`
  - This is the main resume script that likely does the actual restore logic.

- [gcode_macro RESUME_INTERRUPTED]
  - This is the main recovery routine.
  - It reads:
    - `power_resume_z` from saved variables
    - `last_file` from saved variables
  - Then it:
    - clears Z offset
    - sets the virtual position to the saved Z height
    - heats bed and nozzle
    - raises Z a little
    - homes X/Y
    - runs the shell script `POWER_LOSS_RESUME`
    - starts the print again with:
      - `SDCARD_PRINT_FILE FILENAME=plr/"{last_file}"`
  - This means after a power loss, it tries to resume the print from the previously saved printable file.

- [gcode_macro LOG_Z]
  - Reads the current Z position
  - `SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=power_resume_z VALUE={z_pos}`
  - So every time you manually log Z, it saves the current layer height to recover from.

- [save_variables]
  - `filename = {USER_HOME}/printer_data/config/variables.cfg`
  - This is where the saved values are stored.

Important point:
- This config is not a normal Klipper â€œresumeâ€ feature; it is a custom PLR implementation using shell scripts and saved variables.
- It assumes the print file is inside a folder named `plr` on the SD card or print storage.

A few things to double-check in this config:

- `PLR_DIR` must be defined elsewhere in your printer config, or this command will fail.
- The script path `/home/rangarajan/printer_data/plr/plr.sh` must exist and be executable.
- `clear_plr.sh` must also exist if `G31` calls it.
- `SDCARD_PRINT_FILE FILENAME=plr/"{last_file}"` means the resumed file must actually be under the `plr` subfolder.
- `SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION` is powerful but can be dangerous if the machine position is incorrect; it is often used only after a verified power-loss event.

If you want, I can also explain:
- exactly how the power-loss resume sequence works step by step
- which lines to change if you want to use it on your printer
- whether this config is correct for your printer setup or has any mistakes

Copilot: Yes â€” from your file, this is a Klipper Power Loss Resume (PLR) setup, and the main logic is in `plr.cfg`.

What it does:
- [gcode_shell_command clear_plr]
  - Runs a shell script when `G31` is called.
  - This is likely a â€œclear PLR stateâ€ command.

- [gcode_macro save_last_file]
  - Saves:
    - the last printed file name
    - the full file path
  - into persistent variables using `SAVE_VARIABLE`

- [gcode_macro clear_last_file]
  - Clears those saved values back to empty strings

- [gcode_macro RESUME_INTERRUPTED]
  - This is the important resume macro:
    - reads saved `power_resume_z`
    - reads `last_file`
    - heats bed and nozzle
    - lifts Z slightly
    - homes X/Y
    - runs a shell script `plr.sh`
    - then restarts the file with:
      - `SDCARD_PRINT_FILE FILENAME=plr/"{last_file}"`

- [gcode_macro LOG_Z]
  - Saves the current Z height into `power_resume_z`
  - This acts as the â€œresume Zâ€ point after a power loss

So this config is essentially:
- log current print position before power loss
- save file name
- on resume, restore Z, set temps, and continue the print

A few important notes from your config:
- `command: /bin/bash /home/rangarajan/printer_data/plr/plr.sh`
  - This path is hardcoded to your machine. Thatâ€™s okay if it is always the same printer, but it is not portable.
- `timeout: 420.`
  - For Klipper, integer seconds are usually safer. `420` is better than `420.` in some setups.
- `SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=last_file VALUE='"{ filename[-1] }"'`
  - This can sometimes produce odd quoting. It usually works, but if it misbehaves, the saved value may include extra quotes.
- `RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=POWER_LOSS_RESUME PARAMS="{z_height} \"{last_file}\""`
  - If `last_file` is empty, your script can get a malformed argument string. You may want a guard for that.

The most likely behavior of your system:
- PLR saves the last file and Z height
- after a power loss, you call `RESUME_INTERRUPTED`
- the script restores print, then resumes the file from the correct place

User: Klipper command errors and states chat unable to use u can read the full chat

Copilot: I can only see the current Klipper config snippet, not the earlier chat history, but based on `plr.cfg`, the most likely causes of â€œcommand errors / state issuesâ€ are these:

1. `timeout` is written as a float
- In your config:
  - `timeout: 5.`
- Klipper expects a numeric value, but this is commonly treated poorly as a float.
- Safer:
  - `timeout: 5`

2. Your quotes are fragile in SAVE_VARIABLE and RUN_SHELL_COMMAND
- This pattern is risky:
  - `SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=last_file VALUE='"{ filename[-1] }"'`
  - `PARAMS="{z_height} \"{last_file}\""`
- Klipper/Jinja quoting is easy to break here, especially when paths or file names contain spaces or quotes.
- The macro should be kept simple and predictable.

3. `clear_plr` runs a shell script but there is no check if it exists or is executable
- This is a common source of â€œcommand failedâ€ or â€œshell command errorâ€ messages:
  - `command: sh {PLR_DIR}/clear_plr.sh`
- If `{PLR_DIR}` is not defined in config, it will fail.

4. `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` calls a shell script with a positional parameter
- This is a common place for errors:
  - `RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=POWER_LOSS_RESUME PARAMS="{z_height} \"{last_file}\""`
- If `last_file` is empty, or contains spaces, the script may receive bad arguments.
- You should validate `last_file` before running the script.

5. `SDCARD_PRINT_FILE FILENAME=plr/"{last_file}"`
- This assumes the file is always in a folder named `plr/`
- If the saved file is just a filename without path, this is okay.
- If the value contains a full path, this can fail.

Recommended safe version
Use this cleaned-up version in your macro:

```ini
[gcode_shell_command clear_plr]
command: sh {PLR_DIR}/clear_plr.sh
timeout: 5

[gcode_macro save_last_file]
gcode:
    {% set filepath = printer.virtual_sdcard.file_path %}
    {% set filename = filepath.split('/')[-1] %}

    SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=last_file VALUE='"{filename}"'
    SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=filepath VALUE='"{filepath}"'
    M118 Last File: {filename}

[gcode_macro clear_last_file]
gcode:
    SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=last_file VALUE='""'
    SAVE_VARIABLE VARIABLE=filepath VALUE='""'

[gcode_shell_command POWER_LOSS_RESUME]
command: /bin/bash /home/rangarajan/printer_data/plr/plr.sh
timeout: 420

[gcode_macro RESUME_INTERRUPTED]
gcode:
    {% set z_height = printer.save_variables.variables.power_resume_z|float %}
    {% set last_file = printer.save_variables.variables.last_file|string %}
    {% set bed_temp = 55 %}
    {% set extruder_temp = 190 %}
    {% set z_lift = 5 %}
    {% set dwell_ms = 0 %}

    {% if last_file == "" %}
        RESPOND MSG="No last file saved. Resume aborted."
        RESTART
        return
    {% endif %}

    SET_GCODE_OFFSET Z=0 MOVE=0
    SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION Z={z_height} SET_HOMED=XYZ
    M140 S{bed_temp}
    M190 S{bed_temp}
    M104 S{extruder_temp}
    M109 S{extruder_temp}
    G4 P{dwell_ms}
    G91
    G1 Z{z_lift} F600
    G90
    G28 X Y
    M118 {last_file}
    RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=POWER_LOSS_RESUME PARAMS="{z_height} {last_file}"
    SDCARD_PRINT_FILE FILENAME=plr/{last_file}
```

Important:
- The `PARAMS` string should not include nested quotes unless your shell script specifically expects them.
- If your `plr.sh` script expects one argument like `z_height` and another like `last_file`, it is often better to keep them separate and avoid quoting complexity.

Also, if you are getting â€œcommand not foundâ€ or â€œunknown commandâ€ errors, check:
- klippy.log
- `printer.cfg` syntax
- whether `gcode_shell_command` is enabled in your build
- whether `clear_plr.sh` and `plr.sh` actually exist and are executable

If you want, I can next help with one of these:
- debug the exact error from your log
- rewrite the full PLR flow cleanly
- explain each macro in your file line by line
- make `plr.sh` compatible with this config

Not all the resume steps are inside `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` in the active [plr.cfg](<C:/Users/ranga/Downloads/config-20260804-202716/config/14/printer_data/config/plr.cfg>).

The active macro checks the saved recovery state, sets `z_lift`, runs the shell command, then starts the generated recovery file. The actual recovery sequenceâ€”bed preheat, nozzle heat, bed target reset, Z lift, XY homing, and resume positioningâ€”is generated by [plr.sh](<C:/Users/ranga/Downloads/config-20260804-202716/config/14/printer_data/plr/plr.sh>).

So the current setting `{% set z_lift = 2 %}` in the active config is passed to the script and controls the lift. The macro in [KlipperPLR/plr.cfg](<C:/Users/ranga/Downloads/config-20260804-202716/config/14/KlipperPLR/plr.cfg>) is only a commented reference; Klipper does not load it.

User: in RESUME_INTERRUPTED temperature ?

Copilot: In the active `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` macro, **there are no temperature settings**. It passes the saved Z, filename, and lift value to `plr.sh`.

The script generates the recovery G-code, which:

1. Heats the bed to **95% of the G-codeâ€™s first-layer bed temperature** and waits.
2. Heats the nozzle to the **normal print temperature from the G-code** and waits.
3. Sets the bed target to the **normal bed temperature** from the G-code, then continues recovery.

So the temperatures come from the printâ€™s G-code, not fixed values in [plr.cfg](<C:/Users/ranga/Downloads/config-20260804-202716/config/14/printer_data/config/plr.cfg>). When the resumed G-code continues, its own later temperature commands may change the targets.

Ran terminal command: @'
import re, sys, tempfile
from pathlib import Path
root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\14')
active_script = (root / 'printer_data' / 'plr' / 'plr.sh').read_text()
template_script = (root / 'KlipperPLR' / 'plr.sh').read_text()
marker_active = "python3 - \"$filepath\" \"$resume_z\" \"$output_path\" \"$excluded_names\" <<'PY'\n"
marker_template = "python3 - \"$filepath\" \"$resume_z\" \"$output_path\" \"$excluded_names\" <<'PY'\n"
active_body = active_script.split(marker_active, 1)[1].split('\nPY\n', 1)[0]
template_body = template_script.split(marker_template, 1)[1].split('\nPY\n', 1)[0]
compile(active_body, 'active-plr-generator', 'exec')
compile(template_body, 'template-plr-generator', 'exec')
assert active_body == template_body, 'Active/template generator bodies differ'
source = root / 'printer_data' / 'gcodes' / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_3m50s.gcode'
lines = source.read_text(errors='replace').splitlines()
number = r'[+-]?(?:\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+)'
z_re = re.compile(rf'(?:^|\s)Z\s*({number})(?=\s|$)', re.I)
saved_z = next(float(m.group(1)) for line in lines if line.lstrip().upper().startswith(('G0 ', 'G1 ')) for m in [z_re.search(line.split(';', 1)[0])] if m)
outputs = []
for body in (active_body, template_body):
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        output = Path(temp_dir) / source.name
        sys.argv = ['plr-generator', str(source), str(saved_z), str(output), 'CUBE_ID_0']
        exec(compile(body, 'plr-generator', 'exec'), {})
        generated = output.read_text().splitlines()
        assert not any(line.startswith(('M140 ', 'M190 ', 'M104 ', 'M109 ', 'G28 ', 'SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION', 'SET_GCODE_OFFSET')) for line in generated)
        assert not any(re.match(r'^G1 Z(?:2|5)\s', line) for line in generated)
        assert generated[0] == 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE RESET=1'
        assert 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT NAME=CUBE_ID_0' in generated
        assert any(line.startswith('G1 X') for line in generated)
        assert any(line.startswith('G1 Z') for line in generated)
        outputs.append(generated)
assert outputs[0] == outputs[1]
macro = (root / 'printer_data' / 'config' / 'plr.cfg').read_text()
commands = ['RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=POWER_LOSS_RESUME', 'SET_GCODE_OFFSET Z=0 MOVE=0', 'SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION Z={z_height} SET_HOMED=XYZ', 'M140 S{bed_first_layer_temp}', 'M190 S{bed_first_layer_temp}', 'M104 S{extruder_temp}', 'M109 S{extruder_temp}', 'G4 P{dwell_ms}', 'M140 S{bed_normal_temp}', 'G1 Z{z_lift} F600', 'G28 X Y', 'SDCARD_PRINT_FILE']
positions = [macro.index(command) for command in commands]
assert positions == sorted(positions), positions
for key, value in [('bed_first_layer_temp', '60'), ('bed_normal_temp', '55'), ('extruder_temp', '190'), ('z_lift', '2'), ('dwell_ms', '0')]:
    assert f'{{% set {key} = {value} %}}' in macro
print(f'PASS: both generators match and generate continuation-only recovery G-code; active macro owns preheat, normal-bed target, lift and homing in order. Fixture saved Z={saved_z:g}.')

Ran terminal command: @'
import re, sys, tempfile
from pathlib import Path
root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\14')
active_script = (root / 'printer_data' / 'plr' / 'plr.sh').read_text()
template_script = (root / 'KlipperPLR' / 'plr.sh').read_text()
marker = "python3 - \"$filepath\" \"$resume_z\" \"$output_path\" \"$excluded_names\" <<'PY'\n"
active_body = active_script.split(marker, 1)[1].split('\nPY\n', 1)[0]
template_body = template_script.split(marker, 1)[1].split('\nPY\n', 1)[0]
compile(active_body, 'active-plr-generator', 'exec')
compile(template_body, 'template-plr-generator', 'exec')
assert active_body == template_body, 'Active/template generator bodies differ'
source = root / 'printer_data' / 'gcodes' / 'Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_3m50s.gcode'
lines = source.read_text(errors='replace').splitlines()
number = r'[+-]?(?:\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+)'
z_re = re.compile(rf'(?:^|\s)Z\s*({number})(?=\s|$)', re.I)
saved_z = next(float(m.group(1)) for line in lines if line.lstrip().upper().startswith(('G0 ', 'G1 ')) for m in [z_re.search(line.split(';', 1)[0])] if m)
outputs = []
for body in (active_body, template_body):
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
        output = Path(temp_dir) / source.name
        sys.argv = ['plr-generator', str(source), str(saved_z), str(output), 'CUBE_ID_0']
        exec(compile(body, 'plr-generator', 'exec'), {})
        generated = output.read_text().splitlines()
        assert not any(line.startswith(('M140 ', 'M190 ', 'M104 ', 'M109 ', 'G28 ', 'SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION', 'SET_GCODE_OFFSET')) for line in generated)
        assert not any(re.match(r'^G1 Z(?:2|5)\s', line) for line in generated)
        assert generated[0] == 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE RESET=1'
        assert 'EXCLUDE_OBJECT NAME=CUBE_ID_0' in generated
        assert any(line.startswith('G1 X') for line in generated)
        assert any(line.startswith('G1 Z') for line in generated)
        outputs.append(generated)
assert outputs[0] == outputs[1]
macro = (root / 'printer_data' / 'config' / 'plr.cfg').read_text()
commands = ['RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=POWER_LOSS_RESUME', 'SET_GCODE_OFFSET Z=0 MOVE=0', 'SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION Z={z_height} SET_HOMED=XYZ', 'M140 S{bed_first_layer_temp}', 'M190 S{bed_first_layer_temp}', 'M104 S{extruder_temp}', 'M109 S{extruder_temp}', 'G4 P{dwell_ms}', 'M140 S{bed_normal_temp}', 'G1 Z{z_lift} F600', 'G28 X Y', 'SDCARD_PRINT_FILE']
positions = [macro.index(command) for command in commands]
assert positions == sorted(positions), positions
for key, value in [('bed_first_layer_temp', '60'), ('bed_normal_temp', '55'), ('extruder_temp', '190'), ('z_lift', '2'), ('dwell_ms', '0')]:
    assert f'{{% set {key} = {value} %}}' in macro
print(f'PASS: both generators match and generate continuation-only recovery G-code; active macro owns preheat, normal-bed target, lift and homing in order. Fixture saved Z={saved_z:g}.')

number = r'[+-]?(?:\d+(?:\.\d*)?|\.\d+)'

    ('PLA', text, 55.0, 190.0),
    ('ABS', text.replace('; hot_plate_temp = 55', '; hot_plate_temp = 100').replace('; nozzle_temperature = 190', '; nozzle_temperature = 245').replace('; hot_plate_temp_initial_layer = 60', '; hot_plate_temp_initial_layer = 110').replace('; nozzle_temperature_initial_layer = 200', '; nozzle_temperature_initial_layer = 255'), 100.0, 245.0),

        out = Path(temp_dir) / 'recovery.gcode'

Assistant: Added a calculated nozzle preheat target and restored the normal nozzle target after waiting. Warned that a hotter nozzle may cause extra oozing and should remain within printer/filament limits.

E. TEMPERATURES MUST COME FROM EACH PRINT'S G-CODE

User: Bed and nozzle temperatures 55 and 190 must be taken from the failed print file; PLA is fine, but ABS needs its own G-code temperatures.

- After copying updates to the printer, save config and run `RESTART` before attempting recovery. Verify generated recovery G-code before relying on it.

from pathlib import Path

root = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config')
source = root / 'Chat copy.txt'
destination = root / 'PLR chat history - organized.txt'
old_chat = source.read_text(encoding='utf-8-sig', errors='replace').rstrip('\n')

summary = r'''KLIPPER PLR CHAT HISTORY AND CHANGE GUIDE
========================================

Purpose
-------
This guide combines the original exported chat with the later follow-up discussion. It puts the latest PLR design and the reasons for changes first, so you can understand the current setup without reading every troubleshooting step. The complete original chat is preserved verbatim in the final section.

CONTENTS
--------
1. Current setup at a glance
2. How a recovery run works
3. Change history: what changed and why
4. Which files matter and where to edit
5. Important safety / deployment notes
6. Follow-up chat highlights
7. Original chat export, verbatim


1. CURRENT SETUP AT A GLANCE
============================

Workspace snapshot reviewed: folder `14`.

Active files in the snapshot:
- `14\printer_data\config\plr.cfg` â€” active Klipper config. This is the config intended to be edited in Mainsail.
- `14\printer_data\plr\plr.sh` â€” active recovery-file generator used by the shell command.
- `14\KlipperPLR\plr.cfg` â€” installer/template config; its `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` macro is commented out as a reference.
- `14\KlipperPLR\plr.sh` â€” installer/template generator for future installs.

Current behavior in folder 14:
- `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` validates the saved interrupted-print state, then calls `POWER_LOSS_RESUME` and starts the generated recovery file.
- The active macro passes editable values to the script: bed preheat factor `1.05`, nozzle preheat factor `1.05`, Z lift `2 mm`, and dwell `0 ms`.
- The script reads normal bed and nozzle targets from the interrupted print's original G-code. This supports different materials and slicer profiles (for example, PLA versus ABS).
- The generated recovery G-code establishes the saved Z position; heats and waits for bed/nozzle at 105% of the normal G-code targets; restores the normal targets; dwells; lifts Z; homes X/Y; restores object-exclusion and extrusion/fan state; then continues at the selected safe resume move.
- The generator refuses to proceed if it cannot identify valid temperatures or a safe resume position, rather than silently guessing.

Example temperatures (illustrative):
- G-code normal targets bed 55 C / nozzle 190 C -> preheat bed 57.75 C / nozzle 199.5 C, then restore 55 C / 190 C.
- G-code normal targets bed 100 C / nozzle 245 C -> preheat bed 105 C / nozzle 257.25 C, then restore 100 C / 245 C.

Important architecture clarification:
The user asked to put all `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` behavior in the active config. The editable controls and orchestration are in the active macro, but actual setup G-code is emitted by `plr.sh`. This split is deliberate in the current snapshot: the script reads arbitrary G-code to discover material-specific temperatures, and the macro cannot directly parse that file. Do not assume the macro itself contains every generated heater/motion command. The commented template macro is a reference and is not active.


2. HOW A RECOVERY RUN WORKS
===========================

1. Earlier print macros save the current file path/name and Z position in Klipper's saved variables.
2. In Mainsail, run `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` (without temperature arguments).
3. The macro checks that interruption state, file path/name, and saved Z are present.
4. It calls `RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=POWER_LOSS_RESUME` with the saved Z, filename, preheat factors, lift, and dwell.
5. `plr.sh` reads the original G-code and saved object-exclusion state, finds the next safe resume layer/travel position, and writes a recovery G-code file under `gcodes/plr/`.
6. That generated file contains the recovery setup and continuation. The macro starts it with `SDCARD_PRINT_FILE`.

`RUN_SHELL_COMMAND CMD=POWER_LOSS_RESUME` is normally called by the macro; it need not be run separately unless deliberately testing generation. If testing it alone, check that a recovery file was created before trying to resume.


3. CHANGE HISTORY: WHAT CHANGED AND WHY
=======================================

A. Initial failure: no recovery file and homing errors
- Reported: `Error running command {POWER_LOSS_RESUME}`, no recovery G-code in `gcodes/plr`, and `Must home axis first`.
- The generator initially assumed a layer-height Z move also had X/Y coordinates. Actual slicer output used Z-only moves, so the script could fail to locate a resume move and stop before creating the file.
- Generator logic was changed to find the next higher layer and then a non-extruding XY travel move, avoiding replay of an extrusion segment.
- Log review later found a separate shell execution problem: Klipper tried to execute `plr.sh` directly and received `PermissionError`. The command was changed to invoke `/bin/bash` explicitly.
- The homing error was traced in a log to a separate `gcode_button` callback. It was not the same failure as the PLR script error.

B. Recovery motion order and macro controls
- User required bed heat and wait first, then nozzle heat and wait, an adjustable zero-second dwell, a small Z lift, and XY homing.
- The resume setup was made configurable, with the lift and dwell as constants rather than command-line tuning each time.
- In later revisions, the macro was expected to run with only `RESUME_INTERRUPTED`; it calls the shell generator itself.

C. Temperature handling evolved
- Early version used fixed PLA-like values (55 C bed, 190 C nozzle). This would not be suitable for every material.
- The design was changed to read the normal bed/nozzle targets from the print's G-code, rather than use first-layer nozzle temperature or fixed PLA temperatures.
- User then requested a 5% hotter preheat. The current folder-14 design applies 1.05 multipliers to each normal target, waits, then restores normal targets from that same print G-code.
- The current editable factors are passed from the macro; temperature extraction and generated commands are in the script.

D. Object-exclusion preservation
- The recovery generator was updated to restore `EXCLUDE_OBJECT_DEFINE` metadata, currently excluded objects, and the active object marker. This prevents a resumed multi-object print from forgetting excluded-object state.
- Exclusions are persisted with Klipper saved variables and cleared when a new/normal print flow resets them.

E. Active config versus template
- `printer.cfg` includes `plr.cfg`; therefore Mainsail's `printer_data/config/plr.cfg` is the active config.
- `KlipperPLR/plr.cfg` is a template used by the installer, not the live config merely because it exists in the source folder.
- The installer originally copied over the active config on install/update. It was changed to keep an existing active config so Mainsail edits are not silently overwritten.
- At the user's request, the template's `RESUME_INTERRUPTED` macro was kept as a fully commented reference to avoid duplicate macro definitions.

F. Folder-14 final adjustment in this follow-up
- Bed and nozzle normal values were changed from constants to values read from the failed print's G-code.
- Both preheat factors are currently 1.05; current Z lift is 2 mm and dwell is 0 ms.
- Active and template script generator bodies were tested with PLA-like and ABS-like values. The test confirmed the targets were read from each G-code, preheated by 5%, restored to normal, and followed by the expected lift/home sequence.


4. WHICH FILES MATTER AND WHERE TO EDIT
=======================================

For normal use through Mainsail:
- Edit `14\printer_data\config\plr.cfg` for the preheat factors, Z lift, dwell, and macro orchestration.
- The active `14\printer_data\plr\plr.sh` performs G-code parsing and builds the recovery file. It must be deployed to the printer if it differs from the printer copy.
- The `14\KlipperPLR\` files are source/template files for installation and future maintenance; editing them alone does not immediately update the printer's active files.
- After config edits, run `RESTART` and wait for Klipper to become ready. A script-only update generally does not require Klipper config reload, but verify that the printer has the intended script copy.

Current macro settings in folder 14 are:
- `bed_preheat_factor = 1.05`
- `nozzle_preheat_factor = 1.05`
- `z_lift = 2`
- `dwell_ms = 0`

Normal heater targets are read from the failed print's G-code, not set as fixed 55/190 constants in the current design.


5. IMPORTANT SAFETY / DEPLOYMENT NOTES
======================================

- A 5% hotter nozzle target may cause extra oozing and may exceed the safe temperature for a filament or printer. Check the printer's configured heater limit and material recommendations before recovery. The generator should fail if it cannot parse normal targets; do not bypass such a failure by guessing.
- The examples above show arithmetic, not an assurance that every printer/material supports those targets.
- Never test recovery motion while a person or object is in the printer's motion path. Confirm XY homing behavior and safe Z clearance for the actual machine.
- Workspace edits are not automatically copied to the physical printer. Confirm the active config and script on the printer before relying on them.
- The original transcript includes some intermediate instructions and older defaults that were superseded. Prefer this guide's â€œCurrent setupâ€ section and the actual active files in the printer's current deployment.


6. FOLLOW-UP CHAT HIGHLIGHTS (NOVEMBER 11-12, 2026)
===================================================

import subprocess, sys, tempfile
from pathlib import Path
project = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\KlipperPLR-Custom-Anycubic-Kobra-2-Neo')
source = Path(r'c:\Users\ranga\Downloads\config-20260804-202716\config\14\printer_data\gcodes\Cube_0.2mm_PLA_Generic Klipper Printer_3m50s.gcode')
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as td:
    temp = Path(td)
    variables = temp / 'variables.cfg'
    output = temp / 'gcodes' / 'plr_custom'
    variables.write_text('[Variables]\nfilepath = ' + repr(str(source)) + '\nlast_file = \'"' + source.name + '"\'\npower_resume_z = 0.44\nplr_excluded_objects = \'""\'\n', encoding='utf-8')
    result = subprocess.run([sys.executable, str(project / 'plr.py'), 'generate', '--variables', str(variables), '--output-dir', str(output), '--z', '0.44', '--file', source.name], text=True, capture_output=True)
    if result.returncode:
        print(result.stdout)
        print(result.stderr)
        raise SystemExit(result.returncode)
    generated = (output / source.name).read_text(encoding='utf-8').splitlines()
    assert generated[:2] == ['SET_GCODE_OFFSET Z=0 MOVE=0', 'SET_KINEMATIC_POSITION Z=0.44 SET_HOMED=XYZ'], generated[:2]
    assert 'M140 S57.75' in generated and 'M190 S57.75' in generated

    assert 'M140 S55' in generated and 'M104 S190' in generated
    assert 'G1 Z2 F600' in generated and 'G28 X Y' in generated
    print(result.stdout.strip())
    print(f'PASS: real fixture generated {len(generated)} recovery lines with 55/190C targets and 5% preheat.')

Ran terminal command: $log='c:\Users\ranga\.copilot\session-state\05b82012-8721-4a40-98e2-f2859cd0d80a\events.jsonl'; @'
import json, sys, datetime
path = sys.argv[1]
counts = {}
starts = {}
calls = []
users = []
def parse_time(value):
    if not value:
        return None
    base = datetime.datetime.strptime(value[:19], '%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%S')
    fraction = 0.0
    if len(value) > 20 and value[19] == '.':
        digits = ''.join(ch for ch in value[20:] if ch.isdigit())[:6]
        if digits:
            fraction = int(digits) / (10 ** len(digits))
    return base + datetime.timedelta(seconds=fraction)
with open(path, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as stream:
    for line in stream:
        try:
            event = json.loads(line)
        except Exception:
            continue
        kind = event.get('type')
        counts[kind] = counts.get(kind, 0) + 1
        data = event.get('data') or {}
        if kind == 'user.message':
            users.append({'timestamp': event.get('timestamp'), 'content': data.get('content')})
        elif kind == 'tool.execution_start':
            starts[data.get('toolCallId')] = {'tool': data.get('toolName'), 'timestamp': event.get('timestamp')}
        elif kind == 'tool.execution_complete':
            start = starts.get(data.get('toolCallId'), {})
            elapsed = None
            try:
                elapsed = (parse_time(event.get('timestamp')) - parse_time(start.get('timestamp'))).total_seconds()
            except Exception:
                pass
            calls.append({'tool': start.get('toolName', start.get('tool', 'unknown')), 'success': data.get('success'), 'durationSeconds': elapsed})
print('EVENT_COUNTS', json.dumps(counts))
print('USER_MESSAGES', json.dumps(users, ensure_ascii=False))
print('TOOL_CALLS', json.dumps(calls, ensure_ascii=False))
'@ | python - $log

        elif e.get('type')=='tool.execution_complete' and not d.get('success',True):
            name,timestamp=starts.get(d.get('toolCallId'),('unknown',None))
            failures.append({'tool':name,'timestamp':e.get('timestamp'),'result':str(d.get('result'))[:500]})

Assistant: The clone creates `~/KlipperPLR-Custom-Anycubic-Kobra-2-Neo`.
The README and installer were corrected to use that canonical repository URL
and directory name.

User: Retried with the correct directory, but `./install.sh` returned
`Permission denied`.

Assistant: The script is tracked without the executable bit. Use
`bash ./install.sh`; the README was updated to show that command.

User: Ran the installer successfully. It reported `Installation complete`.
After uninstalling, the repository directory remained, so another `git clone`
reported that the destination already existed. Running `bash ./install.sh`
from that existing directory still completed.

Assistant: Confirmed that the active config include and installed PLR files
were present. Recommended running `RESTART` in Mainsail and waiting for
Klipper to report Ready.

2. MOONRAKER COULD NOT START

User: Mainsail reported it could not connect to Moonraker.

Assistant: Read-only checks showed Moonraker repeatedly failing on a stale
`[include update_plr.cfg]` directive because that file did not exist.
The installed custom updater config was `update_plr_custom.cfg`.

User: Backed up `moonraker.conf`, commented out only the missing legacy include,
and restarted Moonraker. The service became active, but the first immediate
local API check returned HTTP `000`.

Assistant: Explained that an active service does not guarantee its API has
finished starting and requested a later status and listening-port check.

User: Follow-up checks showed Moonraker listening on port 7125 and the API
returned HTTP `200`. The logs also showed Klipper could not open
`/dev/serial/by-id/usb-1a86_USB_Serial-if00-port0`.

3. PRINTER USB / MCU CONNECTION

User: Checked configured and available serial devices. The config expected
`/dev/serial/by-id/usb-1a86_USB_Serial-if00-port0`, but `/dev/serial`,
`/dev/ttyUSB*`, and `/dev/ttyACM*` were all absent.

User: `lsusb` listed the Raspberry Pi root hubs, a VIA Labs hub, and an OPPO
realme GT 6T, but no printer serial adapter.

Assistant: Concluded that Moonraker was reachable, but Klipper could not
connect to the printer because the Pi was not detecting its USB serial
device. Recommended checking printer power and a data-capable USB cable,
not guessing a serial path or flashing firmware, and not attempting recovery
until Klipper is ready.

4. REVIEW OF `Pi 4 Data\3` AND COMMANDS

Assistant: Checked the uploaded snapshot. `printer.cfg` includes
`plr_custom.cfg`; `moonraker.conf` includes `update_plr_custom.cfg` and has
the missing legacy `update_plr.cfg` include commented out; the updater file
uses the canonical repository URL and path; and the custom PLR files exist.
Moonraker's earlier include errors were historical, before the fix. The
snapshot still showed the missing MCU serial device.

The README was updated locally with the fresh-install commands:

```sh
cd ~
git clone https://github.com/rangarajan604/KlipperPLR-Custom-Anycubic-Kobra-2-Neo.git
cd ~/KlipperPLR-Custom-Anycubic-Kobra-2-Neo
bash ./install.sh
cd ~
```

For an existing checkout, do not clone again:

```sh
cd ~/KlipperPLR-Custom-Anycubic-Kobra-2-Neo
git pull --ff-only
bash ./install.sh
cd ~
```

`cd ~` returns to the home directory; `cd .` leaves the current directory
unchanged. The README changes are local workspace edits and were not pushed
to GitHub during this discussion.

No printer motion or heater test was performed. Do not use
`RESUME_INTERRUPTED` until the printer USB device is detected and Klipper
reports Ready.
